Looking at the actual PIDs running on bjb1-18228, I see 474319 is still unaccounted for, but 452987 and 428411 are close matches to the registered PIDs 452982 and 428406 respectively—likely child processes spawned from the parent jobs.

I'm also seeing 428122 on GPU-06a5, which corresponds to the argmin-seed44-r2 job. So across the four GPUs on this machine, I have the perdof-seed44 experiment still running on cuda:1, obs-only-norm02-seed44-r2 on cuda:2, and argmin-seed44-r2 on cuda:3, with an unknown process taking up cuda:0. The P0 experiments appear to still be active.

Now checking bjb1-32546, which has all six GPUs occupied. The registry shows scalar-seed44-r2 on cuda:0, vla-mean-seed44 on cuda:1, obs-only-norm03-seed44 on cuda:2, and obs-only-norm02-seed45 on cuda:3, with the remaining two GPUs also in use.

Moving to bjb2-28827, which also has all six GPUs active. I'm verifying the process mappings here—scalar-seed45 is running on cuda:0, perdof-norm02-seed45 on cuda:1, and I need to confirm the assignments for the obs-only and vla-only variants across the remaining GPUs. I'm seeing the same experiments continuing on bjb2-28827 with the eval variants, and there are two unregistered PIDs on cuda:2 and cuda:3 that might be the obs-only experiments mentioned earlier. On bjb2-40593, there's one GPU running what looks like the perdof-default-seed44 experiment. The critical observation is that all experiments across both servers are still actively running—none have finished yet, including the P0 priority experiments.

Now I need to check the JSON output files to see if there's any recent activity or completion signals, and verify that these PIDs are actually the eval processes and not child processes with mismatched IDs.
